# Two actual growing tree-simplex projection bodies

Status: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max, round53.
Assignment TASK.md and master_guidance8e797e97b78dcebd authorize ONE
analytical round after completed52/attempted53, ceilings53/54. No formal
verification or fact_submit is authorized or performed. No new fact_id.

Two concrete bodies are selected, of the permitted maximum three. The
first has a rigorous scoped full-word obstruction. The second has actual
face changes, an explicit all-query state rule and a priced full-word
interface, but no proved useful terminal payoff. A representation or
first-H2 calculation is not claimed to solve that interface. All new
ingredients are proved jointly here; earlier unverified findings are
awareness only. No mathematical evaluator, numerical run, solver, DP
execution, machine algebra, replay, installation, consult, or subagent
was used.

## 1. Original words and complete reference scopes

Throughout fix an integer m>=128 and set

 lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
 N=2^m, n=N-1, h=H_m, S=sum_(i=0)^(n-1)h_i.

Every child retains these SAME enclosing-m parameters. In particular
h_0=1+a, h_1=1+beta, h_2=1+a. Counting each level gives

 1+S=N+(beta/c)(c^m-N)/(c-2)
     =(1-zeta)N+zeta*c^m<c^m,
 zeta=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).                         (1.1)

Indeed c>lambda and lambda(lambda-2)=1, whereas beta<1.
For i>=0 let s2(i) be the number of ones in its binary expansion, with
s2(0)=0, and define the signed original prefix product

 Pi_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t)=(-1)^i beta^i c^(-s2(i)). (1.2)

To prove the identity, the original recursive word has
h_t-1=beta*c^(v2(t+1)-1), where v2(k) is the exponent of2 in k.
Counting multiples of powers of2 shows
sum_(k=1)^i v2(k)=sum_(j>=1)floor(i/2^j)=i-s2(i): for a binary
digit at position j its contribution to the floor sum is2^j-1.
Multiplication gives (1.2), including i=0. Consequently

 |Pi_i|<=a for 1<=i<=n,
 Qunit:=Pi_n^2=beta^(2n)c^(-2m).                    (1.3)

For a body K used below, the actual project function is f=L F_K and
the actual iterates obey x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad F_K(x_i). Define

 V_K=2F_K(x_n)/R_K^2,
 R_K=dist(x_0,argmin F_K).                           (1.4)

This is the fixed W=2R normalization. The sharp global smoothness of
each selected F_K is1, so no smaller local constant is substituted.

The accepted reference facts used are the following.

* b98bb807d514e80d: the COMPLETE single-cut functional

 K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S), max_(0<=j<n)
  (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+sum_(t<j)h_t)^2(1+2sum_(t>j)h_t)]}.

  It also specifies the ORIGINAL all-split recurrences

 C_0=B_0=1,
 C_t=max_(0<=k<t)Omega(C_k,C_(t-k-1)),
 B_t=max_(0<=k<t)G(C_k,B_(t-k-1)),
 Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
 G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
 U_t^ZJ=1/B_t.

* 1fca3e5dd555cb4a: F_ray_n(h) is its maximum over ALL feasible
  chronological checkpoint subsets, with the original retained offsets,
  value areas, same-ray monotonicity conditions and terminal q optimization.
  Its complete class contains K_n(h). No restricted checkpoint list is
  substituted here. The only comparison needed below is

 Huber(h):=1/(1+2S)<=K_n(h)<=F_ray_n(h).              (1.5)

* dfde2fa326c908bf: Poly_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z),
  xi_i=Poly_i(z), gamma_i=z Poly_i(z), together with the zero optimum.
  The FULL legal domain D_h consists of all z=alpha+i beta_complex
  such that (alpha-|z|^2) Energy_C>=2|beta_complex Area_C| for EVERY
  directed simple cycle of these original query indices and optimum;
  Energy_C=sum|xi_i-xi_j|^2 and
  Area_C=sum Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i) on the oriented edges j->i.
  If ell_i is the maximal path weight from i to the optimum for weights
  <gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2/2 on j->i, its exact maximal
  gap is Gap_h(z)=-2ell_n. Write

 Cfull(h)=sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z).

  At z=1 all cycle conditions hold, and the upper and lower gap bounds
  of that accepted fact coincide. Hence

 Cfull(h)>=Gap_h(1)=Qunit.                           (1.6)

  This comparison uses a point in the FULL domain, not a claim that a
  lens or real slice exhausts it.

* 04219554e41d9750, proof(9.3), gives for the same original recurrence
  B_t<=4(t+1)^(log_2 lambda), in particular B_n<=4lambda^m. Its full
  statement and the proof's indices were read directly from the fact
  file. No new recurrence computation or finite-H6 transfer is used.

Thus Qunit<c^(-2m)<1/(2c^m)<Huber(h), by (1.1) and c^m>2.

## 2. Projection function, smoothness, minimum, and all original rows

Let K be any nonempty compact convex subset of a Euclidean space with
0 in K. Define the SAME finite function at every query by

 F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.

Completing the square shows that its unique maximizing vector is the
Euclidean projection g=P_K(x). Existence follows from compactness and
uniqueness from strict convexity of squared distance. The exact normal
condition is

 <x-g,v-g><=0 for every v in K.                     (2.1)

Necessity follows by differentiating squared distance on the segment
from g to v. Sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-v||^2-||x-g||^2.
Adding (2.1) for two inputs proves
<x-x',g-g'>=||g-g'||^2, hence ||g-g'||<=||x-x'||.
The maximizing formulas at x and x+d give

 0<=F_K(x+d)-F_K(x)-<g,d><=<P_K(x+d)-g,d><=||d||^2.

Therefore F_K is finite, convex, differentiable, and grad F_K=P_K is
globally1-Lipschitz. Also

 2F_K(x)=||x||^2-||x-P_K(x)||^2.                    (2.2)

Its minimum is0, and its WHOLE minimizer set is

 K^polar={x:<x,v><=0 for all v in K}.                (2.3)

One direction follows directly from the maximizing formula. In the other
direction, if <x,v>>0 for some v in K, a sufficiently small positive
multiple epsilon*v is in K and has positive objective. This proves
that x is not a minimizer. It also proves (2.3) without identifying the
minimum with an arbitrarily selected point.

Write y_i=x_i-g_i, f_i=F_K(x_i), with g_i=P_K(x_i). The original
interpolation convention in accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Substitution of f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 gives the exact identity

 Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                            (2.4)

The inequality is (2.1). It holds for ALL directed pairs, however
distant, and with any actual minimizer sample g_*=0,f_*=0. No sampled
values, remote rows, or terminal rows are fitted separately.

Both bodies below have nonempty Euclidean interior in their stated
ambient space. On their interiors P_K(x)=x, so their global smoothness
constant1 is sharp.

## 3. The explicit growing tree and exact symmetry reduction

Set

 r_tree=2/c, Zgeom=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)r_tree^j,
 w_j=r_tree^j/Zgeom, 0<=j<m.                        (3.1)

Use one orthonormal coordinate e_v for every binary word v of length
less than m. For a leaf s=(s_1,...,s_m) define

 a_s=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)sqrt(w_j)(-1)^(s_(j+1))e_(s_1...s_j).
                                                                    (3.2)

There are N leaves and N-1 coordinates. Each ||a_s||=1 and sum_s a_s=0.
Distinct coordinate rows of the feature matrix are orthogonal: disjoint
supports give zero, and every proper descendant row cancels within an
ancestor's constant-sign region. Every row has positive squared norm.
Thus the matrix has rank N-1, its kernel is exactly the constant leaf
vector, and the N features are affinely independent. In particular0 is
in the interior of their simplex. If two leaves first diverge at depth k,

 <a_s,a_t>=sum_(j<k)w_j-w_k.                       (3.3)

Let s_left=0^m, write a_left=a_(s_left), and let e_root=e_empty be the
root coordinate. The selected initial points have coordinates only on its
spine, and possibly one common carrier. The group of tree automorphisms
within every off-spine subtree acts orthogonally, fixes these points,
and permutes the leaf features. Projection onto either selected body
commutes with this group by uniqueness. Every non-spine coordinate of a
fixed vector is zero: swapping the children of its node reverses that
coordinate. The GD update therefore preserves this subspace at EVERY
original physical query, not just at word checkpoints.

Average the features in each first-right-turn group k, 0<=k<m, and
retain the all-left leaf as group m. Their spine vectors are

 b_k=sum_(j<k)sqrt(w_j)e_(0^j)-sqrt(w_k)e_(0^k), k<m,
 b_m=sum_(j<m)sqrt(w_j)e_(0^j).                     (3.4)

An off-spine group average has exactly these coordinates, since every
later coordinate cancels. Averaging any convex representation over the
group shows that the centered simplex's fixed section is EXACTLY
conv{b_0,...,b_m}. With a carrier, its fixed section is the convex hull
of0 and the corresponding carrier plus each b_k.

Here is an explicit constructive active-set rule, valid for every state
in this section, including every future state below. For a finite listed
simplex with vertices v_k, consider a nonempty subset I of its vertices.
Choose k0 in I, form the edge columns E_I=(v_k-v_k0)_(k in I\{k0}),
and set

 t_I=(E_I^T E_I)^(-1)E_I^T(x-v_k0),
 g_I=v_k0+E_I t_I.                                (3.5)

For a singleton take g_I=v_k0. The edge Gram matrix is positive definite
by affine independence. Every entry is specified by (3.1),(3.4) and the
chosen carrier; no unknown feature or Gram fitting is present. Retain a
subset exactly when its barycentric coefficients t_I and1-sum t_I are
nonnegative and

 <x-g_I,v_l-g_I><=0 for every listed vertex l.       (3.6)

At least one subset qualifies: use the positive barycentric support of
the true projection. Its within-face first derivatives vanish, giving
(3.5); its remaining inequalities are (2.1). Conversely any qualifying
subset gives the unique projection by (2.1). Degenerate supports can
repeat the same answer but cannot give different answers. Equations
(3.5),(3.6) are a finite symbolic characterization; no enumeration or
numerical evaluation was run. They specify all subsequent physical GD
queries by x_next=x-h*g and their values by the SAME F_K. The quantitative
conclusions below require additional analysis beyond this rule.

## 4. Candidate one: a common carrier stays in a screened cone regime

Add an orthogonal unit coordinate e_car and put

 kappa=sqrt(w_0/3), v_s=kappa e_car+a_s,
 K_car=conv({0} union {v_s}),
 C_car={sum_s t_s v_s:t_s>=0},
 v_left=v_(s_left), x_0=v_left.                     (4.1)

The v_s are linearly independent: a zero combination first has coefficient
sum0 by its carrier coordinate, then all coefficients zero by affine
independence of the a_s. Hence K_car has full dimension N and C_car is
a closed convex cone (an invertible linear image of the nonnegative
orthant). A vector in C_car has a unique total mass

 mass(g)=g_car/kappa=sum_s t_s.

It belongs to K_car exactly when mass(g)<=1. By (2.3), the whole minimum
set is C_car^polar. Because x_0 is in C_car, for any z in this polar
<x_0,z><=0, and ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2. Thus0 is the actual closest
minimum and the EXACT paid radius is

 R_car^2=1+kappa^2.                                 (4.2)

The first gradient is g_0=x_0, so x_1=-a x_0. Define the uniform
right-root subtree average

 u_R=kappa e_car-sqrt(w_0)e_root.

The actual next gradient is

 g_1=(a/2)u_R,      ||g_1||=a*kappa.                 (4.3)

Here is a full normal check. Set y_1=x_1-g_1. For every leaf whose
first bit is1, <y_1,v_s>=0, because
<v_left,v_s>=-2w_0/3 and <u_R,v_s>=4w_0/3. For a different leaf
first diverging from s_left at depth k>=1,

 <y_1,v_s>=-a(sum_(j<k)w_j-w_k)<0,

since w_k<=w_1<w_0. For s=s_left the inner product is -a<0.
Thus y_1 is in C_car^polar and <y_1,g_1>=0. For any v in C_car,
<y_1,v-g_1><=0, proving P_C_car(x_1)=g_1. Moreover its mass is
a/2<1, so (4.3) is also P_K_car(x_1). The mixture is origin plus
ALL right-root leaves, uniformly within that subtree. It is derived,
not prescribed. The noncore state y_1 is nonzero; for example every
deeper left-spine coordinate is -a sqrt(w_j).

We reproduce the precise cone mapping needed for the full word, rather
than using the earlier unverified C37 finding as a premise. For any
closed convex cone C and u=P_C(x), put z=x-u. Applying (2.1) to0 and2u
gives <z,u>=0 and z in C^polar. Conversely
<u,w-z><=0 for all w in C^polar, so z=P_(C^polar)(x). Therefore

 ||P_C(x)||=dist(x,C^polar),
 F_C(x)=||P_C(x)||^2/2,
 dist(x-hP_C(x),C^polar)<=|1-h|dist(x,C^polar).      (4.4)

The last bound uses the same old point z in the next distance minimization,
since x-hu=z+(1-h)u. The cone maximum defining F_C is finite by completing
the square, even though C itself is unbounded.

Run the cone dynamics from the actual x_1 and denote its projection norms
by d_i. By (4.3),(4.4),(1.2), for every i>=1,

 d_i<=a*kappa*product_(1<=t<i)|1-h_t|
     =kappa |Pi_i|<=a*kappa<kappa.                  (4.5)

Each cone projection has mass at most d_i/kappa<1. It is therefore in
K_car and is ALSO its projection. Induction identifies the entire cone
trajectory with the entire actual bounded-body trajectory. In particular
every original physical query and the terminal value come from the SAME
F_K_car. No changed function is substituted after query1. Formula (3.5)
with the carrier vertices, or the proved cone normal condition, selects
all faces without any cancellation/extinction requirement.

This trajectory has another genuine face change within the first H2.
At x_2=x_1-(1+beta)g_1, every right-root leaf has inner product
-2a beta w_0/3<0, whereas a leaf first diverging at depth1 has

 <x_2,v_s>=a w_0(r_tree-1+beta/3)>0.               (4.6)

For the last strict inequality it suffices to prove
6m^2>lambda(2m+1)(m+1). Using lambda<5/2 reduces this to
m^2>(15/2)m+5/2, which holds at m=8 and increases thereafter.
Some small positive multiple of this left vertex has positive objective,
so g_2 is nonzero. A mixture supported solely on the right-root leaves
and origin has nonpositive linear term and cannot attain this positive
objective. Thus g_2 has positive left-root mass. No assertion is made
that the right-root mass vanishes or that a fixed next face persists.

Despite these changing mixtures, (4.5) yields a COMPLETE full-word screen:

 0<=V_car<=kappa^2/(1+kappa^2)*Qunit
          =w_0/(3+w_0)*beta^(2n)c^(-2m).            (4.7)

Since 0<w_0/(3+w_0)<1, equations(1.5),(1.6) give the strict SAME-word
comparisons

 V_car<Qunit<Huber(h)<=K_n(h)<=F_ray_n(h),
 V_car<Cfull(h),
 B_n V_car<=4*w_0/(3+w_0)*(lambda/c^2)^m
                      *beta^(2n) ->0.              (4.8)

For the limit, lambda/c^2<1/lambda<1. Thus this entire explicit
unbounded-m family is weak, even though its faces change and its normal
state is retained. It has been mapped to the previously screened
squared-distance/cone class with its TRUE minimum distance. The radial
cap has zero multiplier at x_0 and is strictly inactive at every later
projection. Per the assignment, this candidate stops here. This is not
an upper bound for arbitrary initial points of K_car, other carrier
sizes, other truncations, or general simplices.

## 5. Candidate two: remove the carrier and retain the actual mixtures

Select the centered body with no carrier

 K_ctr=conv{a_s:s in {0,1}^m}, x_0=a_(s_left).        (5.1)

Adding an explicit origin to this convex hull changes nothing. Section3
shows0 is an interior point, so (2.3) gives the UNIQUE minimum0 and

 R_ctr^2=1.                                        (5.2)

The cone generated by K_ctr is the whole ambient space. At query1 the
finite boundary really matters, as demonstrated below, so the cone
mapping from Section4 cannot be carried over.

Write the actual spine coordinates as x_(i,j)=sqrt(w_j) z_(i,j).
If p_(i,k) are the unique barycentric masses of g_i in the vertices b_k,
define q_(i,j)=sum_(k>=j)p_(i,k). Then q_(i,0)=1 and
1=q_(i,0)>=q_(i,1)>=...>=q_(i,m)>=0, and

 d_(i,j)=2q_(i,j+1)-q_(i,j),
 g_(i,j)=sqrt(w_j)d_(i,j),
 z_(i+1,j)=z_(i,j)-h_i d_(i,j), z_(0,j)=1.          (5.3)

At every query the masses are exactly selected by (3.5),(3.6) with b_k.
Equivalently they uniquely minimize
sum_(j<m)w_j[z_(i,j)-(2q_(j+1)-q_j)]^2 on the displayed monotone
q simplex. The constructive rule, not this optimization notation alone,
specifies the answer. The exact values and normalized terminal score are

 F_K_ctr(x_i)=sum_(j<m)w_j[z_(i,j)d_(i,j)-d_(i,j)^2/2],
 V_ctr=sum_(j<m)w_j[2z_(n,j)d_(n,j)-d_(n,j)^2].      (5.4)

These are a persistent m-dimensional state and every original physical
step. There is no smaller reset state or new child clock.

## 6. Actual first-H2 projections for the centered body

Define for positive integers J

 Dsp_J=sum_(j=0)^(J-1)(2c)^j,
 sigma_3=(7a-1)/Dsp_3,
 sigma_4=(15a-2-beta)/Dsp_4.                        (6.1)

For m>=128 the elementary bounds

 2/5<a<5/12, 12/5<c<5/2, 127/128<=beta<1,
 1/20<sigma_3<1/15, 1/52<sigma_4<1/40              (6.2)

hold. The bounds on a,c follow from 12/5<lambda<29/12 and m>=128.
For sigma_3 its numerator is between9/5 and23/12, and its denominator
is between721/25 and31; in particular
9/155>1/20 and (23/12)/(721/25)=575/8652<1/15.
For sigma_4 the numerator is greater than3 and less than10/3; its
denominator is less than156 and greater than17429/125>400/3.

Initially g_0=x_0 and z_(1,j)=-a for EVERY depth. The exact projection
at query1 is

 d_(1,j)=-a+sigma_3 c^j, j=0,1,2,
 d_(1,j)=0, j>=3.                                  (6.3)

Its positive masses are exactly exit groups0,1,2. To verify this, take

 q_(1,1)=(1-a+sigma_3)/2,
 q_(1,2)=a-sigma_3 c^2, q_(1,3)=...=q_(1,m)=0.

The relation q_(1,3)=0 is equivalent to the definition of sigma_3,
and (5.3) gives (6.3). Moreover

 7/24<q_(1,1)<1/3, 0<q_(1,2)<1/20.

For the second bounds its numerator over Dsp_3 is
a(1+2c)+(1-3a)c^2. The numerator is at least
58/25-25/16=303/400>0, and at most337/250;
division by Dsp_3>721/25 gives an upper bound337/7210<1/20.
Consequently p_0=1-q_1,p_1=q_1-q_2,p_2=q_2 are all positive.

The residual y_1 has scaled coefficients -sigma_3 c^j for j<3
and -a for j>=3. Because r_tree*c=2, its inner product with b_0,b_1,b_2
is the common value w_0 sigma_3. Its inner product with b_3 is
w_0[-7sigma_3+a r_tree^3]. This is strictly smaller, because
sigma_3>a/c^3: the left side exceeds1/20 and a/c^3<1/30.
For subsequent first-exit vertices the residual inner products strictly
decrease: an increment inside the constant negative tail is
-a w_k(2-r_tree)<0. The final all-left vertex also has a smaller value
than its immediately preceding exit vertex, by -2a w_(m-1)<0.
Thus every normal condition(3.6) holds and the displayed projection is
the actual unique one.

The next ACTUAL state, after the original h_1=1+beta, is

 z_(2,j)=a beta-(1+beta)sigma_3 c^j, j=0,1,2,
 z_(2,j)=-a, j>=3.                                 (6.4)

Its next projection has positive masses exactly on exit groups0,1,2,3:

 d_(2,j)=z_(2,j)+sigma_4 c^j, 0<=j<4,
 d_(2,j)=0, j>=4.                                  (6.5)

To verify, note
1+sum_(j<4)2^j z_(2,j)=2+beta-15a,
so the definition of sigma_4 gives q_(2,4)=0. Define earlier q by
q_(2,0)=1 and q_(2,j+1)=(q_(2,j)+d_(2,j))/2.
All four barycentric masses are strictly positive, as follows.
The bounds(6.2) give

 1/4<d_(2,0)<1/2, 0<d_(2,1)<1/4,
 d_(2,2)=-a+(1+beta)q_(1,2)+sigma_4 c^2<-23/160<0,
 d_(2,3)=-a+sigma_4 c^3<-3/320<0.

For completeness, the lower bounds on the first two entries follow
from 127/320-2/15>1/4 and127/320-1/3>0. Their upper bounds follow
from5/12+1/40<1/2 and
5/12+1/16-(255/128)(1/20)(12/5)<1/4.
It follows that 5/8<q_(2,1)<3/4 and
p_1=(q_(2,1)-d_(2,1))/2>0, while p_0=1-q_(2,1)>0.
Since q_(2,4)=0, q_(2,3)=-d_(2,3)>0 and
p_2=q_(2,2)-q_(2,3)=q_(2,3)-d_(2,2)>0; finally p_3=q_(2,3)>0.

The residual at query2 has scaled coefficients -sigma_4 c^j for j<4
and -a thereafter. Its values on the four active vertices are all
w_0 sigma_4. The first inactive normal inequality is exactly
sigma_4>=a/c^4, which is strict because sigma_4>1/52 and
a/c^4<1/75. The negative tail then makes all further normal values
strictly decrease, with the same last-leaf check. This proves(6.5).

The common active normal values also give the exact actual conjugate
excesses cex_1=<y_1,g_1>=w_0 sigma_3>0 and
cex_2=<y_2,g_2>=w_0 sigma_4>0. Thus the values from the SAME function are

 F_K_ctr(x_0)=1/2,
 F_K_ctr(x_1)=(1/2)sum_(j<3)w_j(-a+sigma_3 c^j)^2+w_0 sigma_3,
 F_K_ctr(x_2)=(1/2)sum_(j<4)w_j d_(2,j)^2+w_0 sigma_4.

No free height or separate value assignment is used. The residuals are
nonzero at both queries, including their retained deep components.

The third physical update in the first H2 is therefore exactly

 z_(3,j)=z_(2,j)-(1+a)d_(2,j).                       (6.6)

For every j>=4 it is STILL -a. Equations(6.3)-(6.6) are actual
projections and updates; they do not prescribe a rank-one law. In
particular the face changes between queries1 and2, and the full deep
noncore state is retained. At query3 and every subsequent query the
all-support rule(3.5),(3.6),(5.3) applies. No assertion that these four
groups remain active is made.

## 7. Two direct continuation attempts and their limits

The first attempt was to propagate a fixed exposed face, or treat a
visit to the quadratic core as permanent. Both shortcuts fail on these
very data. Equations(6.3),(6.5) give different positive supports at
queries1 and2. Also x_0 lies in K_ctr, but x_1=-a a_left does NOT.
Its unique full-leaf barycentric coefficients are

 p_s=(1+a)/N for s!=s_left,
 p_(s_left)=(1+a)/N-a<0.                            (7.1)

The strict inequality follows from a>2/5 and N>=4. Consequently
P_K_ctr(x_1)!=x_1. An initial core visit already fails to persist through
the next original step. No remote interpolation defect occurs: (2.4)
holds, and the true projection supplies the required repair automatically.

The second attempt was to simplify the full simplex projection by
thresholding its unconstrained leaf masses with the ordinary Euclidean
probability-simplex formula. This is false for the selected Haar metric.
Applying that threshold formula to (7.1) would give mass1/(N-1) on all
non-left leaves, mass0 on the left leaf, and hence

 g_wrong=-a_left/(N-1).

All those non-left leaves would have positive weights, so their normal
inner products with y_wrong=x_1-g_wrong must be identical at a genuine
projection. The values for leaves first diverging at depths0 and1 differ
by

 (-a+1/(N-1))*(2w_0-w_1),                          (7.2)

which is nonzero. This directly violates the within-face stationarity
condition. The inverse feature map changes the Euclidean metric; ordinary
thresholding is not invariant under that change of coordinates.

These are concrete failures of two attempted reductions, not a proof
that a competitive centered-tree trajectory is impossible. In particular
there is no proof that the support remains a prefix of exit groups after
query2, and no failure of such a later-only prefix conjecture is asserted.
The full state rule retains arbitrary later supports.

## 8. Full-word accounting and a quantitative conditional screen

The centered candidate has a fully paid initial radius, but its terminal
estimate remains substantive. Here is exact full-word accounting that
retains the noncore state. Set

 y_i=x_i-g_i, cex_i=<y_i,g_i>>=0.

Nonnegativity follows from (2.1) with v=0. Since
x_(i+1)=(1-h_i)g_i+y_i, direct expansion gives

 ||x_(i+1)||^2=(1-h_i)^2||x_i||^2
   +h_i(2-h_i)||y_i||^2-2h_i(h_i-1)cex_i.

None of the Pi_i is zero. Dividing by Pi_(i+1)^2 and telescoping gives
the exact quantitative terminal identity

 V_ctr=Pi_n^2*[1+sum_(i<n)
  {h_i(2-h_i)||y_i||^2-2h_i(h_i-1)cex_i}/Pi_(i+1)^2]
  -||y_n||^2.                                     (8.1)

It uses every original physical query. The bracket is not set to1.
For h_i>=2 both displayed contributions are nonpositive. For 1<h_i<2
the squared-residual contribution can be positive, and its size must
be controlled jointly with the negative terms and the terminal residual.
For this candidate the exact benchmark-matching requirement is

 Pi_n^2*[1+sum_(i<n)
  {h_i(2-h_i)||y_i||^2-2h_i(h_i-1)cex_i}/Pi_(i+1)^2]
  -||y_n||^2 >=1/B_n.                              (8.2)

No inequality(8.2), useful constant fraction of it, or converse for all
m has been proved. Equation(8.1) is an identity, not a lower bound.

A second direct continuation attempt uses a rigorously testable core
criterion instead of assuming persistence. If at an actual power-of-two
query k=2^j with 1<=j<=m-1 one has

 x_k in K_ctr and -x_k/c in K_ctr,                  (8.3)

then every later query is exactly in the core, and

 x_i=(Pi_i/Pi_k)x_k for k<=i<=n,
 V_ctr=||x_k||^2 beta^(2(n-k))c^(2-2m).             (8.4)

Indeed Pi_k=beta^k/c is positive. For even i>=k the ratio Pi_i/Pi_k
lies in[0,1], since s2(i)>=1. For odd i>=k the bit count is at least2
(the only odd positive integer with a single one is1), so the ratio
lies in[-1/c,0]. Convexity and(8.3) put every candidate iterate in K_ctr.
Induction then gives its actual gradient equal to itself at each step.

This criterion has a quantitative full-word consequence. Every gradient
of F_K_ctr has norm at most1, since it is a convex combination of unit
features. Hence ||x_k||<=1+sum_(t<k)h_t<=1+S<c^m. From(8.4), for any
k in the stated range,

 V_ctr<c^2 beta^(2(n-k))<=c^2 exp(-2(n-k)/m),
 B_n V_ctr<4lambda^m c^2 exp[-(2^m-2)/m] ->0.       (8.5)

We used log(1-1/m)<=-1/m and k<=2^(m-1). The limit follows since
2^m/m^2 tends to infinity; for example its ratio at consecutive m is
2(m/(m+1))^2, eventually at least3/2. This is a CONDITIONAL screen for
actual states satisfying(8.3), not a statement that(8.3) occurs. It is
not inferred from a single core membership or from the first-H2 data.

The first direct attempt at a global law failed by the exact face and
core counterexamples. The independent mass-threshold attempt failed
by(7.2). The remaining route is to control the true active-set evolution
in(5.3), or establish(8.3), or obtain a competitive lower estimate in
(8.2). All are genuine all-history estimates. Replacing them by a fixed
label, exact extinction, discarded tail, or an unproved induction would
change the construction.

## 9. Literature and resource record

Search stage: active_program. Program: actual growing tree-simplex
projection dynamics. Missing mechanism: an analytic all-query active-set
law with a paid terminal comparison. Mode: repair/mutation. Intent:
construction and projection-background; no whole-goal impossibility search.

Matlas searches were made for tree/simplex projection and a precise
threshold statement. The first returned no applicable tree projection
theorem (its tropical-metric and unrelated statistical results do not
apply). The precise second search returned cone normal conditions from
arXiv:2107.07257, Lemma9, and standard-simplex thresholding from
arXiv:2204.08153, Proposition2.1. Neither is imported as a black-box result.

The exact primary paper read is Weiran Wang and Miguel A. Carreira-Perpinan,
"Projection onto the probability simplex: An efficient algorithm with a
simple proof, and an application", arXiv:1309.1541, 2013, Sections1-3
and Theorem1 with proof. Its problem is min||p-y||^2/2 subject to p>=0,
sum p=1 in the STANDARD Euclidean probability coordinates. It characterizes
p_i=max(y_i+lambda_threshold,0), with the threshold fixed by the sum and
an explicit sorted-support test. The relevant proof idea is equality of
normal derivatives on the positive support, used directly in(3.5),(3.6)
and to refute the unjustified transfer in(7.2). The paper's scalar
threshold itself does not apply to the non-isometric Haar feature map.

Local files: downloads/max53_simplex_projection/1309.1541.pdf, .html,
and .txt. The PDF and HTML were downloaded. An already present local
pypdf installation extracted the text, and the relevant extracted pages
were read in bounded sections. Extraction warned that fontTools was
missing; nothing was installed. The defining equations and proof were
also read in the exact arXiv HTML, so no formula depends on an unchecked
font-encoding extraction. Source URL: https://arxiv.org/html/1309.1541.

Search is background only; every new mathematical ingredient used here
has a joint elementary derivation in this manuscript. No external result
is a correctness premise, and no formal submission was made.

Readback/retrieval disclosures: initial broad memory/tool output was

were located. A notation-cleanup patch failed its context match, made

not search snippets or old local drafts,
were used for accepted benchmark scopes.

Resources used for mathematics: zero host mathematical evaluations,

# Centered tree projection: fixed discovery batch, max54

Status: all five authorized jobs completed and passed their registered
numerical checks. Discovery only; NO verifier call, NO fact_submit,
NO new fact. The original exact-profile Goal remains OPEN. This closes
the ONE round allocated after completed53/attempted54; actual completion
of this worker turn will be recorded by the outer loop after return.

Authority: TASK.md and latest master_guidance5017191b944783b7.
Prior centered construction aa3c5e1d91baf405 and shortcut warning
b671acaef6faf790 were recalled, not imported as accepted facts.
Full preregistration is PLAN.md; self-contained analytic prerequisites
are DERIVATION.md. Those files and both source files were hashed before
the first mathematical import and remained unchanged through all jobs.

## 1. Frozen object and actual function

For the enclosing integer m set beta=1-1/m,
c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+1/m), a=beta/c, r_tree=2/c, and
w_j=r_tree^j/sum_(k<m)r_tree^k. Set H_0=() and
H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j); every child keeps the same m.
The physical length is n=2^m-1. Each full leaf feature is the signed
sum of sqrt(w_j) times its orthonormal internal-node coordinates.
K is the centered convex hull of all2^m leaf features, and
F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2).

The actual initial point is the all-left leaf. The joint derivation
proves that the full feature matrix has rank2^m-1, the leaves form a
simplex with0 in its interior, grad F_K=P_K, smoothness1 is sharp,
the unique minimizer is0, and the true initial squared distance is1.
Thus the reported normalized value is V=2F_K(x_n)/(L R^2)=2F_K(x_n).
The actual GD step is x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i P_K(x_i), with L=1.

Off-spine automorphisms leave the trajectory invariant. Their fixed
section is exactly the m-dimensional simplex with vertices b_k: positive
sqrt(w_j) coordinates before the first-right exit k, negative sqrt(w_k)
at k, zero later; b_m is the all-left leaf. A residual in this spine has
the same normal on every full leaf in an exit group as on its b_k.
Every group weight lifts to a uniform nonnegative mixture of that group's
leaves. Thus reduced barycentric feasibility and all m+1 reduced normals
are precisely the original full-body conditions, not a substituted metric.

DERIVATION.md gives the weighted q-coordinate objective and two independent
inverse barycentric formulas. Ordinary probability-simplex thresholding
is not used. Every query receives a fresh core test. A core identity is
used only at that tested input; no suffix is skipped and no permanent
core claim is inferred from an entry time or the two-sided diagnostics.

## 2. Calibration and all-query diagnostics

Job1 used m2 with all4 full leaf features in the original3-dimensional
space. Its independent solver enumerated all15 nonempty full faces at
each of the4 queries, using augmented Gram/equality systems. The reduced
solver used affine-edge Cholesky systems and its core shortcut was disabled.
The two trajectories evolved independently. Maximum discrepancies:

* state: 2.1510571102112408e-16;
* gradient: 4.163336342344337e-16;
* actual function value: 9.107298248878237e-18;
* initial squared radius against1: 1.1102230246251565e-16.

All selected full and reduced projections passed feasibility, normality
and positive-support stationarity. The calibration's actual trajectory
is entirely in the core; it does not independently exercise a full-body
exterior projection. The larger cases do exercise exterior projections,
and each is checked against all original normals through the exact
symmetry reduction. The independent m8 run below further checks them.

Binary64 maximum positive normal residuals were
1.3655526362454928e-16 (m6), 1.405159896859104e-16 (m8), and
2.0116016057752928e-16 (m10). Maximum barycentric sum/nonnegativity
error was1.1102230246251565e-16 in each. Maximum reconstruction error
was below9.7e-17. No case hit a pivot, time, nonfinite or solve-residual
guard. Maximum pivots for one primary projection were5,7,9 respectively,
well within the predeclared4(m+1)^2 limits. No primary deletion occurred.

## 3. Terminal values and correctly oriented comparisons

The following entries are saved numerical observations, not certified

## 4. Face and core history relevant to a future full-history bound

Every query including the terminal one was evaluated and logged.

| m | noncore queries | observed final all-core segment | distinct supports |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6 | 1 through7 | 8 through63 | 6 |
| 8 | 1..30,32..37,40 | 41 through255 | 12 |
| 10 | 1 through72 | 73 through1023 | 14 |

These final segments are checked finite histories, not a permanence
theorem. In particular m8 enters the core at31, leaves at32, enters
again at38, leaves again at40, and re-enters at41. Both numerical
precisions agree at every query. At query31 the actual next step is
48.612488871744068...; the minimum represented core mass is about
0.00244371348441130. Nevertheless the next state is outside, with
||y_32||^2=0.0002141183496716297... . At query38 the smallest mass is
about0.000830006766364056, but query40 is again outside with
||y_40||^2=6.859079945932178e-7. Thus even a later core visit with
positive interior margin is insufficient for a permanent-core shortcut.

The later faces are not always prefixes of exit groups. At m8 query8,
the support is exactly the numerically identified groups{0,1,2,3,4,6},
with mass0.040403237538339567... on group6 while group5 has zero mass.
Its inactive normal at group5 is-0.003700780730117329... . Those are
large margins compared with the numerical residuals, and they recur
in the independent high-precision run. The complete nonprefix query
list for m8 is8,16,18,32,33,34,35,36,37,40 (plus initial leaf0's group8).
For m10 it is16,17,18,19,24,72 (plus its initial singleton group10).
This is numerical evidence against a future universal prefix-support
induction; it is not a formally verified counterexample.

The exact forward identity rederived in DERIVATION.md is

 ||x_(i+1)||^2=(1-h_i)^2||x_i||^2
       +h_i(2-h_i)||y_i||^2-2h_i(h_i-1)<y_i,g_i>.

Both signed defect terms are saved at every physical step. At m8 query8
they are respectively +0.006957665854579188... and
-0.000221830220359244...; dropping the noncore residual is unjustified.
At query40 they are +6.147169188795793e-7 and
-9.164753975367975e-7. Their combined sign can change, so the termwise
record supports joint accounting rather than a fixed sign assumption.
The largest discrepancy of the iterated forward norm accounting was
9.965986369486757e-16 in binary64 m10. The stable function computation
does not depend on obtaining a tiny terminal value by subtracting these
large historical quantities.

Fresh two-sided diagnostics x_k in K and -x_k/c in K passed at sampled
power-of-two queries8,16,32 for m6;64,128 for m8;128,256,512 for m10.
For m8 query64, the smallest displayed mass of -x_k/c is about
0.00375109460728257. These saved margins may help a future analytic
entry estimate. This batch does not prove that the criterion occurs
for all sufficiently large m, nor use it to replace any later query.

later supports; a frozen face, scalar mass threshold or first-entry

probability metric from this feature metric. Source:
https://arxiv.org/abs/1309.1541. All mathematical conditions actually
used by the evaluator are rederived in DERIVATION.md.

Operational disclosures: initial broad recall output was truncated and
the decisive draft/guidance/fact portions were reread in bounded sections.
The new ledger initially returned `No such file or directory`; it was
created atomically by job1 before any evaluation. No mathematical run,
calibration, parser or evaluator failed, and no correction job was used.

# Joint analytic prerequisites (UNVERIFIED, max54)

These are rederived here as one draft. No unverified earlier finding is
used as an accepted premise, and no formal gate is authorized this round.
All m used below are integers >=2, so beta,c,a,w are positive.

For each internal node v of the full binary tree of depth m let e_v be
an orthonormal vector. The leaf vector a_s is the sum over its ancestors
at depths j of (-1)^(next bit)*sqrt(w_j)e_v. Its norm squared is sum w_j=1.
Every coordinate row of the leaf matrix has zero sum. Distinct rows are
orthogonal: disjoint supports have zero product; on a nested pair the
descendant's two signs cancel where the ancestor is constant. Each row
has positive norm. The matrix consequently has rank2^m-1 and kernel the
constant leaf vector. Thus the leaves are affinely independent, average0,
and their convex hull K has0 in its full Euclidean interior.

For any x the unique maximizer of <x,g>-||g||^2/2 over compact K is the
minimizer of ||x-g||^2. Its characterization is
g in K and <x-g,v-g><=0 for every vertex v. Necessity follows by the
one-sided derivative along g+t(v-g); sufficiency by expansion of distance.
Adding these inequalities for two projections yields
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>, hence the projection is1-Lipschitz.
The two maximizing formulas sandwich the first-order error of F between
0 and <P_K(x+d)-P_K(x),d>, bounded by||d||^2. This proves convexity,
differentiability and grad F=P_K. On the interior of K, grad F(x)=x,
so smoothness1 is sharp. F>=0. If x!=0, a sufficiently small positive
multiple t*x belongs to K and <x,t*x>-||t*x||^2/2>0. Thus the unique
minimizer is0. At x0=a_(0^m) the true squared minimum distance is1.

Permuting the two children of off-spine tree nodes is an orthogonal signed
coordinate transformation preserving K and fixing every spine vector.
Uniqueness makes projection commute with these transformations. Their
fixed subspace consists exactly of the m coordinates e_(0^j): swapping
the children at any off-spine node negates that coordinate. GD from x0
therefore stays in this subspace at every physical query.

Group leaves by their first right turn k<m, plus the final all-left group
k=m. Averaging a group cancels every off-spine coordinate and gives b_k,
whose j coordinate is sqrt(w_j) for j<k, -sqrt(w_k) for j=k<m,
and0 for j>k; b_m has all coordinates sqrt(w_j). Averaging any convex
representation shows K intersect spine=conv{b_0,...,b_m}. Conversely each
b_k is such an average, so lies in K. They are affinely independent.
For a spine residual y, <y,a_s> depends only on the first-right group and
equals <y,b_k>. Therefore checking all m+1 reduced normals checks every
one of the2^m original full-leaf normals, without assuming sparsity.
A group mass p_k is expanded uniformly over2^(m-k-1) leaves for k<m;
p_m is the last leaf mass. This gives full feasibility from reduced masses.

Write x_j=sqrt(w_j)z_j and let q_j=sum_(k>=j)p_k, with q_0=1.
Then g_j=sqrt(w_j)(2q_(j+1)-q_j), and its exact projection uniquely
minimizes sum_j w_j[z_j-(2q_(j+1)-q_j)]^2 subject to
1=q_0>=q_1>=...>=q_m>=0. This is the true feature metric; ordinary
Euclidean probability-simplex thresholding changes it.

For fresh core membership solve q_(j+1)=(q_j+z_j)/2 and take
p_j=q_j-q_(j+1), p_m=q_m. An independent equivalent formula is
p_k=2^(-k-1)[1+sum_(j<k)2^j z_j-2^k z_k] for k<m,
p_m=2^(-m)[1+sum_(j<m)2^j z_j]. Affine independence proves membership
iff every p is nonnegative. Only at that input does this imply g=x;
it says nothing by itself about the next input or any later face.

For any nonempty candidate face I with base vertex v0 and edge matrix E,
the affine closest point is v0+E t with (E^T E)t=E^T(x-v0).
E has independent columns, so its Gram is positive definite. Alternatively
the barycentric affine solution solves [V^T V,1;1^T,0](p,nu)=(V^T x,1).
This augmented system is nonsingular: a homogeneous solution has1^T p=0
and p^T V^T V p=0, which forces p=0 by affine independence, then nu=0.
An affine solution with nonnegative masses and all vertex normals<=0
is the true projection by the distance expansion. An active-set search
starts feasible, descends to an affine face minimizer, stops at a first
zero weight if needed, and adds a vertex with violated normal. We do
not rely on a complexity/termination theorem: a cap invalidates the case,
and only checked feasible/KKT outputs can be used. The small full-body
calibration instead enumerates its entire finite face list independently.

For y=x-g, F=||g||^2/2+<y,g>, with <y,g>>=0 from the vertex-normal
condition at0. The equality also gives2F=||x||^2-||y||^2. For sampled
jets of this SAME function, the original interpolation convention of
accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 reduces exactly to
Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
    =<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.
Full normals therefore control every past/future directed pair, without
fitting values. Floating-point checks are evidence, not exact certification.

The physical step is h_i=1+a*c^(v2(i+1)), where v2 is the exponent of2.
This follows by induction from H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j).
Every child uses the same enclosing m. Set A_i=||x_i||^2 and cex_i=<y_i,g_i>.
The directly expanded GD identity is
A_(i+1)=(1-h_i)^2 A_i
        +h_i(2-h_i)||y_i||^2-2h_i(h_i-1)cex_i.
The pilot logs both defect terms and all states to locate the remaining
full-history issue. It uses this forward identity for diagnostics, avoiding
division by tiny prefix products. A finite observed final core segment
is not declared permanent for untested inputs or m.

Only two accepted comparisons are evaluated: Huber=1/(1+2sum H_m), from
b98bb807d514e80d (or its predecessor's direct Huber construction), and
B_(2^m-1)<=4(1+sqrt2)^m from04219554e41d9750 proof(9.3).
Thus the latter is an UPPER multiplier, not the exact benchmark B.
No new benchmark recurrence is computed, no full-complex optimum is
estimated, and no analytic all-m terminal estimate is asserted.

# Joint proof of the stated multistage completion theorem

All construction parameters, vectors, lists, functions, channels and benchmark
sequences have the definitions and ranges in the statement. For brevity,
local M,A,B,C,a,p,q,r,Q,R,y,z,w denote the corresponding construction
symbols with subscript mc. A proximal pair means x=u+g with g=P_D_mc(x),
where P_D_mc is Euclidean projection onto D_mc. Partial H denotes the
convex subdifferential of H. In the three-stage terminal calculation, local
L_stage=B+a (written L in the displayed algebra) is a stage coefficient,
not the objective's smoothness constant, which is1 throughout the proof.
The notation E in that calculation means E_3. In the return calculation,
d means d_R. The notation v_0 in each of these separate calculations is
the displacement from its indicated zero-height endpoint to x_n.

The only verified mathematical inputs are fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290
(maximal unqueried-terminal prefix interpolation and signed residual
necessity), fact_id b98bb807d514e80d (the exact all-split benchmark,
K_n, and B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3)), and fact_id dfde2fa326c908bf
(the precise complex-polynomial transcript and universal lens, together
with the reciprocal-root Chebyshev identity and its ellipse estimate).
Their exact definitions are reproduced in the statement. In particular,
the benchmark here ranges over every split and the complex comparison is
only on Lens_n. No audit or unverified finding is a mathematical premise;
all new geometry, terminal active-set algebra and reference comparisons
are established jointly below.

## 1. Static old function and every reached query

Let D=conv{0,p,q,r} and

    F_old(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.

The maximum is attained uniquely because D is compact and the objective
is strictly concave; the maximizer is P_D(x). The projection normal
condition says, for u=x-g,

    <u,b-g><=0 for every b in D.

It also says g maximizes the support J(u)=max(0,<p,u>,<q,u>,<r,u>).
Thus x=u+g, delta=J(u)=<g,u>, and
F_old(x)=delta+||g||^2/2. Conversely, any displayed pair with g in D
and this support condition is the UNIQUE projection pair. Tied support
planes do not make the envelope gradient ambiguous.

For any two old pairs, direct cancellation in the signed original
residual of fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290 gives

    Q_xy=F_old(x)-F_old(y)+<g_x,y-x>+||g_x-g_y||^2/2
        =<u_y,g_x-g_y><=0.

This works for every ordered pair, remote pairs, duplicate positions,
and the zero optimum. To check global regularity directly, the two
projection normal inequalities with each other's projected vectors give

    <x-x',P_D(x)-P_D(x')>>=||P_D(x)-P_D(x')||^2.

Cauchy--Schwarz makes the projection 1-Lipschitz. Evaluation of the
attained maximum at its two maximizing vectors bounds
F_old(x+d)-F_old(x)-<P_D(x),d> between0 and
<P_D(x+d)-P_D(x),d>, hence between0 and ||d||^2. Thus F_old is
differentiable with gradient P_D, which is 1-Lipschitz, and convex as
a supremum of affine functions. The slopes are independent: p has
nonzero first coordinate, q has nonzero second coordinate, and r has
nonzero third coordinate. Therefore D has interior, where P_D(x)=x
and F_old has Hessian I. Its least global smoothness is exactly one.

Because 0 is a vertex of D, F_old>=0 and its complete minimum set is

    {x:<p,x><=0, <q,x><=0, <r,x><=0}.

Indeed this condition makes every objective term nonpositive. If any
of the three inner products is positive, a sufficiently small positive
multiple of that vertex belongs to D and makes the objective positive.
The p inequality puts all minima in x_1<=0; origin is the unique
closest one to e_1 and the true distance is one.

The following table derives the actual static chronology. Its variable
t=1+s, where s is the ordinary mass already spent in the current list;
therefore 1<=t<=M. The right endpoint t=M is also the selected following
bridge query. Nonnegativity of every entry ensures every ordinary
partial mass lies in this interval.

| Stage | Proposed proximal point u | Current support height | Exhaustive other-plane comparisons |
|---|---|---|---|
| p | (1-t/M)e_1 | (1-t/M)/M | q height is minus twice this height; r height equals this height; zero is below it |
| q | X-tq, X=-Ae_1 | (M-t)Q | p height=(-A+2t/M)/M<0; r height=-(M-t)Q/2<=0; zero is below q |
| r | X_R-tr, X_R=y-BMq | (M-t)R | q height=-BMQ+tQ/2<0; p height=(2-A+2B-t/M)/M<0; zero is below r |
| returned q | X_D-tq, X_D=z-CMr | (M-t)Q | r height=-CMR+tQ/2<0; p height=(-A+2t/M)/M<0; zero is below q |

For the first row, the p/r tie holds at EVERY p-stage query. Since p
is an active support subgradient and x=u+p, projection uniqueness
proves the actual gradient is p. It is not necessary to break that tie.
All strict comparisons in the other rows follow from B>=2 and
A>=4B+4, including t=M. At zero height the zero plane may tie with
other planes, but the displayed projection remains unique.

The endpoints and bridges follow by literal GD subtraction:

    after L_0: x=p, u=0;
    p-(AM+1)p = X=-Ae_1;
    y=X-Mq=(2-A,-nu,0),    <q,y>=<r,y>=0;
    (y+q)-(BM+1)q=X_R=y-BMq;
    z=X_R-Mr=y-BMq-Mr;
    <r,z>=0, <q,z>=M(1/2-B)Q, z_1=1-A+2B;
    (z+r)-(CM+1)r=X_D=z-CMr;
    w=X_D-Mq=z-CMr-Mq;
    <q,w>=0, <r,w>=(MQ/2)(1-CB), w_1=2-A.

For h_three the last update is from z+r and reaches z-M epsilon r.
For h_return it is from w+q and reaches w-M epsilon q. Every bridge
is an actual physical query and update. Lists were not collapsed into
oracle calls. A zero entry repeats the same jet and leaves the endpoint
identities unchanged.

At the old three-law terminal z-M epsilon r the first coordinate is
1-A+2B-epsilon<0, the q support is
MQ[(1+epsilon)/2-B]<0, and the r support is -M epsilon R<=0.
At the old return-law terminal w-M epsilon q the first coordinate is
2-A+2epsilon<0, the q support is -M epsilon Q<0, and the r support
is (MQ/2)(1-CB+epsilon)<0. Thus BOTH old terminal values are zero.
Only the unqueried terminal completion creates the stated new payoff.

## 2. Complete conjugate-anchor lists and all prefix rows

For a prefix jet x=u+g, f=delta+||g||^2/2, its conjugate lower
quadratic from fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290 simplifies, with no dropped constant, to

    H_jet(v)=||v||^2/2+<u,v>-delta.

Within one constant-gradient stage both u and delta are affine in
the ordinary partial mass. Thus each intervening affine term is a
convex combination of the first-query and endpoint affine terms;
it cannot enlarge their maximum. BOTH endpoint jets are physical
prefix queries. This proves exact equality of the maximum over every
physical prefix jet with the endpoint maximum, rather than replacing
the interpolation problem by an endpoint relaxation.

Here are all six/eight affine terms, including the original optimum:

| Anchor u | Offset delta | Laws retaining it |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0 | both; p-stage bridge and optimum give the same affine term |
| t_P=a e_1 | a/M | both; initial query |
| y | 0 | both; q-stage ending bridge query |
| t_Q=y+(M-1)q | (M-1)Q | both; FIRST q query |
| z | 0 | both; r-stage ending bridge query |
| t_R=z+(M-1)r | (M-1)R | both; FIRST r query |
| w | 0 | return; returned-q ending query |
| t_D=w+(M-1)q | (M-1)Q | return; FIRST returned-q query |

The affine anchors are distinct. The vectors q,r are independent and
orthogonal to y, while y!=0. In the affine coordinate basis y,q,r,
y,t_Q,z,t_R,w,t_D have respective coordinates
(1,0,0),(1,M-1,0),(1,-BM,-M),(1,-BM,-1),
(1,-(B+1)M,-(C+1)M),(1,-BM-1,-(C+1)M).
These are pairwise different for the stated parameters. Neither0 nor
t_P=a e_1 equals any of them: representation of e_1 in the basis
y,q,r has y coefficient -1/A, so t_P has negative y coefficient,
whereas the six displayed vectors have y coefficient1. The points0
and t_P are distinct because a>0. Thus the distinct affine-anchor
counts are six and eight. In particular the
old FIRST r query is retained after returning to q; its lower quadratic
does not become redundant merely because the endpoint z is retained.

Define H_three as ||v||^2/2 plus the maximum of the first six affine
terms, H_return using all eight, and F_three=H_three^*,
F_return=H_return^*. All ordered old prefix inequalities and the
optimum jet were proved in Section 1. The maximal-prefix construction
of accepted fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290 therefore applies exactly, preserving EVERY old
prefix position, gradient and value, including repeated queries. Its
proof gives finite convex C1 globally 1-smooth conjugates. Fix these
functions before running their words. Induction on the physical indices
then gives the same prefix updates; no online function change is used.

The new terminal gradients are not assumed equal to the old gradients.
At the terminal, x=v+conv(active anchor slopes) is the precise
subgradient optimality condition. It is checked separately below.
Since each completed H is 1-strongly convex, that condition determines
the unique terminal gradient. Incoming AND outgoing terminal rows hold
for the resulting actual function: apply the interpolation necessity in
fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290 to any two of its actual prefix, terminal and
optimum samples. This gives Q_ij<=0 in both orientations for every
ordered pair. The formal Gram is a Gram of the actual vectors in R^3,
hence is positive semidefinite. No queried-prefix comparison was deleted.

## 3. Three-stage terminal, including every boundary

Let L=B+a, E=t_Q-z=M L q+Mr, and

    D_3=||E||^2=2A(L^2-L+B/2),
    v_0=-M epsilon r,
    b_3=<E,v_0>-(M-1)Q=Aa(epsilon-2/M),
    theta_3=(b_3)_+/D_3,    v_3=v_0-theta_3 E.

For a in [1/2,1),
L^2-L+B/2=B^2+B(2a-1/2)+a^2-a>=B^2+B/2-1/4>=B^2.
Thus D_3>=2AB^2 and 0<=theta_3<=epsilon/(2B^2)<=1/8.
This pays the convex-combination range and every denominator sign.

All six affine comparisons can be written explicitly:

* z has value -theta_3<z,E>, with
  <z,E>=2A(1/2-B)L<0. This value is nonnegative, and is
  positive when theta_3>0.
* t_Q minus z has affine value b_3-theta_3 D_3, which is zero
  when b_3>=0 and nonpositive otherwise.
* y and 0 have value zero because y is orthogonal to q and r.
* t_R minus z has value (M-1)(<r,v_3>-R). Here
  <r,v_3>=(MQ/2)(-B epsilon+a theta_3)<0 for epsilon>0.
  At epsilon=0 this difference is instead -(M-1)R<0.
* t_P minus z has value <t_P-z,v_3>-a/M. Directly,
  <t_P-z,v_0>=-a epsilon and
  <t_P-z,E>=2L[A(B-1/2)-a]+a>0, so this difference is negative.

If epsilon<2/M, theta_3=0 and z is active, along with y and 0;
if epsilon=2/M, t_Q also ties but z alone may still be selected.
If epsilon>2/M, z and t_Q are active and theta_3 lies strictly
between zero and one. In every case,

    x_n=z+v_0=v_3+z+theta_3 E in partial H_three(v_3).

At epsilon=0 the terminal gradient and value are zero and z is a
minimum. This covers M=2, epsilon=1 as well, where epsilon=2/M;
there is no hidden positive-theta assumption at that endpoint.

The active z term gives the exact conjugate value by completing a
square:

    V_three=2F_three(x_n)
           =||v_0||^2-(b_3)_+^2/D_3
           =AB epsilon^2-(Aa(epsilon-2/M)_+)^2/D_3.

Since the loss is at most A epsilon^2/(2B^2),

    AB epsilon^2(1-1/(2B^3))<=V_three<=AB epsilon^2.

The formula is continuous across the active-set boundary; it was
derived from the full six-anchor maximum, not a terminal ansatz.

## 4. Return terminal and the retained old first-r query

The feasible return parameter set itself implies M>=2B^2>=8 and
epsilon>0. Epsilon=0 and small M outside this set are not conclusions
of the return theorem. Set

    d=C+a=2B+2-1/M,
    E_R=t_R-w=Mq+Md r,
    Dhat_R=1-d+(B/2)d^2,
    D_R=||E_R||^2=2A Dhat_R,
    v_0=-M epsilon q,
    b_R=<E_R,v_0>-(M-1)R
       =2A[(B-1/(2M))epsilon-Ba/(2M)],
    theta_R=b_R/D_R,    v_R=v_0-theta_R E_R.

The derivative of 1-d+Bd^2/2 is Bd-1>0 in this range and
2B+1<d<2B+2<=3B. The exact value at the lower comparison point is

    1-(2B+1)+(B/2)(2B+1)^2
        =2B^3+2B^2-3B/2.

Since 2B^2-3B/2>0 for B>=2, the displayed exact lower-point
identity and monotonicity give Dhat_R>=2B^3.

The upper bound Dhat_R<=(9/2)B^3 follows by d<=3B and 1-d<0.
Write eta=(B-1/(2M))epsilon-Ba/(2M). The upper eta<=B epsilon
is immediate. For the lower eta>=B epsilon/2 it suffices that
B M epsilon>=epsilon+Ba. But B M epsilon>=2B^3>=B+1>=Ba+epsilon.
Hence

    2B^3<=Dhat_R<=(9/2)B^3,
    epsilon/(9B^2)<=theta_R<=epsilon/(2B^2)<=1/8,

with b_R>0. In particular the active combination is admissible,
including the boundary M epsilon=2B^2 and epsilon=1 when feasible.

The complete eight-anchor screen is as follows.

1. t_R and w tie because their affine difference is
   b_R-theta_R D_R=0. The w value is positive:
   <w,v_0>=0 and <w,E_R>=A d(1-CB)<0.
2. For the old endpoint z, direct substitution, without omitting t_R,
   gives

       <r,v_R>/(Q/2)
          =[M epsilon d(B-1/2)+(Ba/2)(Bd-1)]/Dhat_R.

   Its first numerator term is at least 2B^2 d(B-1/2).
   Since B-1/2>=3B/4 and d<=3B, this is at least B^2 d^2/2.
   Also B Dhat_R<=B^2 d^2/2. The second numerator term is
   strictly positive, so <r,v_R>>R throughout the feasible scope.
   Using the t_R/w tie, the z-minus-w value equals
   (M-1)(R-<r,v_R>)<0. Thus z is inactive even on the stated
   return parameter boundary; this is not presumed from chronology.
3. <q,v_R>=MQ[-epsilon+theta_R(B-1/(2M))]<0, because
   theta_R B<=epsilon/(2B)<epsilon. Thus t_D is below w.
   The t_Q affine value is (M-1)(<q,v_R>-Q)<0.
4. y and 0 have value zero, below the positive w value.
5. For E_P=t_P-w, the independent first-coordinate calculation is

       <E_P,v_0>=2a epsilon,
       <E_P,E_R>=a(d-2)+A d(CB-1)>=4AB^3.

   Indeed d>=2B+1 and CB-1=2B^2+B-1>=2B^2. Consequently
   <E_P,v_R><=2a epsilon-(4AB/9)epsilon<0, since
   A>=4B+4 and B>=2 imply 4AB/9>2. Subtracting delta_P
   makes the t_P-minus-w comparison still more negative.

These cover all eight terms with the original offsets. Since w and
t_R are active and 0<theta_R<1,

    x_n=w+v_0=v_R+w+theta_R E_R in partial H_return(v_R).

The exact value and loss follow from the active w term:

    V_return=2F_return(x_n)=2A epsilon^2-b_R^2/D_R,
    2A epsilon^2(1-1/(2B))<=V_return<=2A epsilon^2,
    V_return>=3A epsilon^2/2.

For the lower value bound, b_R<=2AB epsilon and D_R>=4AB^3
give loss<=A epsilon^2/B. This completes the full feasible parameter
screen, including the epsilon and cap-equality boundaries.

## 5. Complete new minimum sets, attainment and sharp normalization

All positive offsets in the anchor table are strictly positive, since
M>=2, a>0, Q>0 and R>0. At v=0 exactly the zero-offset anchors
are active. The subdifferential of a finite maximum of affine functions
is the convex hull of its active slopes: every such convex combination
is a supporting slope; a slope outside that hull is separated from it
by a direction and cannot satisfy the supporting inequality along that
direction. Therefore

    partial H_three(0)=conv{0,y,z},
    partial H_return(0)=conv{0,y,z,w}.

Each H(0)=0, so its conjugate F>=0. Optimality for the strictly
convex conjugate maximization says F(x)=0 precisely when its maximizer
is v=0, equivalently x in partial H(0). These are the COMPLETE
minimum sets, and the minimum value is attained and is zero.

The first coordinates of y,z,w are respectively 2-A, 1-A+2B,
2-A, all strictly negative. Thus each completed minimum hull lies
in x_1<=0 and contains origin. For any minimum x,
||e_1-x||^2=1-2x_1+||x||^2>=1, with equality only at x=0.
The unique closest minimum is origin and the TRUE radius is exactly
one. Completion shrinks the old minimum cone to these bounded hulls;
radius preservation has been proved from the new hulls, not assumed.

For 0<t<1/M sufficiently small, at v=t e_1 every nonzero
zero-offset anchor has strictly negative affine value, and each
positive-offset anchor has strictly negative affine value too. The
initial one requires t_P,1 t<delta_P, which is exactly t<1/M.
There is an open ball around such v on which the zero affine term
alone wins, so H(v)=||v||^2/2 there. Optimality gives F(x)=||x||^2/2
on that same open ball, with Hessian I. Since global smoothness one
was already supplied by fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290, it is SHARP. There is no hidden
L or distance renormalization. The exact terminal values in Sections
3 and 4 are thus actual attained payoffs in the W_n=2R_n normalization
in R^3.

## 6. Exact growing parameters and physical counts

For each integer N>=2^60 choose

    M=2N(N+1)+1,   A=M^(1/12),   B=M^(1/120),
    epsilon=M^(-1/3),   T_Cheb=(2N+1)^2=2M-1.

M>2^121 implies B>2. Since A=B^10>=2^9 B=512B>=4B+4 for
B>=2, the first bridge hypothesis holds. Also M^(13/20)>2 in this
range, so M epsilon=M^(2/3)>=2M^(1/60)=2B^2. Thus these
are inside the previously screened parameter sets, with epsilon>0.
Accepted fact_id dfde2fa326c908bf gives each Chi_N mass 2N(N+1)/3. Three copies
have mass M-1 and length 3N. The initial list uses three increasing
copies, every later list three decreasing copies. The physical lengths,
including every bridge, are therefore n_three=9N+3 and n_return=12N+4.
The guards in Section 1 cover every entry without executing the lists.

The exact value bounds give

    V_three>=AB epsilon^2(1-1/(2B^3))>=(7/8)M^(-23/40),
    V_return>=3A epsilon^2/2=(3/2)M^(-7/12).

Since B(1-1/(2B^3))>=15/8>3/2, BOTH payoffs obey the common
lower bound V>=(3/2)M^(-7/12). This uses the B factor in V_three,
not only a comparison of power exponents.

## 7. Every original cut and the quadratic/lens channels

With P_j=sum_(i<j)h_i and T_j=sum_(i>j)h_i at the ORIGINAL
physical index, accepted fact_id b98bb807d514e80d defines

    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2sum h),
           max_j (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]}.

Write increasing Chi_N entries as csc^2(k pi/(2N+1)),
k=N,N-1,...,1. If theta=pi/(2N+1), concavity of sine gives
sin((k-1)theta)>=(k-1)sin(k theta)/k. Thus consecutive increasing
entry ratios are at most (k/(k-1))^2<=4. The first entry is <=4/3 for
N>=2, because N pi/(2N+1)>=2pi/5>pi/3. Thus an increasing-copy
entry h is <=9(1+preceding mass in that copy); reversing the copy
gives h<=9(1+following mass in that copy), including the small last
entry. Also sin(pi/(2N+1))>=2/(2N+1) gives max Chi_N<=T_Cheb/4<M.
Within-copy mass remains a lower bound for the corresponding full mass.

The following cut partition is complete. All denominators are at the
physical indices; no schedule compression or mass regrouping is used.

| Physical cut | Bound on its K term | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| ordinary entry in L_0 | 41/(AM) | squared prefix ratio<=81; following mass includes AM+1 |
| any later ordinary entry | 9/(A^2 M) | 1+P_j>=AM; h<=M and h<=9(1+T_j) give h^2/(1+2T_j)<=9M |
| AM+1 | A^2/(2BM) | 1+P_j=M; following mass includes BM+1 |
| BM+1 | B^2/(A^2 M) | 1+P_j>=AM; following mass>=M |
| CM+1, return word | C^2/(A^2 M)<=(25/4)B^2/(A^2 M) | same mass guards; C<=5B/2 |
| final 1+M epsilon | epsilon^2/A^2 | preceding mass>=AM; following mass is zero |
| global Huber term | 1/(2AM) | total mass>=AM |

The last ordinary cut is licensed by DECREASING suffix order. An
increasing final list would not justify this table. After substituting
the parameter powers, the slow possible terms include

    A^2/(2BM)=(1/2)M^(-101/120),
    epsilon^2/A^2=M^(-5/6).

All other displayed terms decay faster than M^(-5/6), with constants
at most 41. Since K is a maximum, BOTH complete words satisfy
K_n(h)<=41M^(-5/6).

Every convex quadratic in any finite dimension diagonalizes, and its
normalized error is a convex combination of t product_j(1-h_j t)^2
for 0<=t<=1. Zero eigenvalues contribute no objective gap; choosing a
farther minimizer only increases the denominator. Consequently the
entire quadratic channel is bounded by
Q_n(h)=max_(0<=t<=1)t product_j(1-h_j t)^2.
Conversely, for each t in (0,1], the scalar quadratic f(x)=t x^2/2
from x_0=1 attains that expression; a constant function gives zero
at t=0. Continuity on [0,1] gives the maximum, so this expression is
exactly the full convex-quadratic channel asserted in the statement.

Let P_N denote the polynomial of one Chi_N copy from fact_id dfde2fa326c908bf. On
[0,1], |P_N(t)|^2<=min(1,1/(T_Cheb t)). The second bound uses
|Cheb_(2N+1)(sqrt(t))|<=1. For the first, set sqrt(t)=sin(phi):
the odd Chebyshev identity reduces to
sin((2N+1)phi)/((2N+1)sin(phi)). The finite unit-complex-number
identity sin(k phi)/sin(phi)=sum_(j=0)^(k-1)
exp(i(k-1-2j)phi) for positive integer k gives
|sin(k phi)|<=k|sin(phi)|; the zero-sine case follows by continuity.
At t=0 the polynomial value is one, which is the meaning of its
displayed min bound at zero.

There are nine Chebyshev copies for h_three, twelve for h_return.
With u=T_Cheb t, the actual bridge coefficients divided by T_Cheb
are <=A,B,1 (three), and <=A,B,C,1 (return). For each
c in {A,B,C}, cM+1<=c(2M-1) follows from c(M-1)>=1, and
1+M epsilon<=2M-1 because M>=2, epsilon<=1. The complete
quadratic expressions are therefore bounded by

    T_Cheb^(-1)u(1+Au)^2(1+Bu)^2(1+u)^2 min(1,u^(-9)),
    T_Cheb^(-1)u(1+Au)^2(1+Bu)^2(1+Cu)^2(1+u)^2
                         min(1,u^(-12)).

For u<=1 use 1+c u<=2c; for u>=1 use 1+c u<=2c u.
The latter products retain u^(-2) and u^(-3), respectively. The
objective at u=0 is zero. T_Cheb>=M and C<=5B/2 give

    Q_n(h_three)<=64 A^2 B^2/T_Cheb<=64M^(-49/60),
    Q_n(h_return)<=256 A^2 B^2 C^2/T_Cheb<=1600M^(-4/5).

This bounds all convex quadratic witnesses, not general scalar nonlinear
or higher-dimensional smooth convex functions.

For the complex comparison the EXACT named universal lens is

    Lens_n={zeta=alpha+i beta:
            alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2))}.

Accepted fact_id dfde2fa326c908bf puts it inside the full cycle-legal region. If
n>=N then Lens_n is also inside Lens_N, because cot(pi/(n+2))
increases with n. The accepted ellipse proof for an N-copy gives
|zeta||P_N(zeta)|^2<=cosh^2(2)/T_Cheb on Lens_N. When
T_Cheb|zeta|<=1, the direct product gives instead

    |P_N(zeta)|<=exp(sum Chi_N |zeta|)<=exp(1/6)<=cosh(2),

since sum Chi_N=(T_Cheb-1)/6. The last constant comparison follows
from exp(1/6)<=sum_(k>=0)(1/6)^k=6/5 and cosh(2)>=1+2=3.
Combining the two regimes, with
Kc=cosh(2), yields

    |P_N(zeta)|^2<=Kc^2 min(1,1/(T_Cheb|zeta|)).

At zeta=0 the polynomial is one and Gap_h(0)=0 because every edge
weight is zero; the min bound is interpreted by that value. At every
nonzero point of the lens the accepted maximal complex transcript obeys
Gap_h(zeta)<=(2alpha-|zeta|^2)|Poly_n(zeta)|^2<=2|zeta||Poly_n(zeta)|^2.
Repeat the preceding product estimate with u=T_Cheb|zeta| to obtain

    sup_(Lens_n) Gap_h_three <=128Kc^18M^(-49/60),
    sup_(Lens_n) Gap_h_return<=3200Kc^24M^(-4/5).

If the channel is augmented by the canonical Huber maximum V_lens,
its extra term is covered in the cut table. These bounds assert NOTHING
about sup_(D_h) Gap_h over the larger schedule-dependent complex
region, or about all scalar functions.

## 8. Original all-split benchmark and actual padded horizons

Here C_n,B_n are benchmark sequences, distinct from construction scalars
C,B. Use EXACTLY the accepted all-split recurrences

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Accepted fact_id b98bb807d514e80d gives B_n<=1+4n^rho_crude, rho_crude=log_2(3). This UPPER
bound is the direction needed to prove reference channels small
relative to U. Since rho_crude<8/5 by 3^5<2^8, M>=2N^2 and actual
n<=16N show

    B_n max(K_n,Q_n,sup_(Lens_n) Gap_h)->0.

The slowest reference decay is M^(-4/5)=O(N^(-8/5)), whereas
B_n=O(N^rho_crude). Fixed cosh(2) constants do not affect the limit.
Thus all three named channels are o(U_n^ZJ) on each selected family
at its actual horizon.

To prove V>U a LOWER bound on B_n is necessary. The all-split
recurrences directly give C_n>=Omega(C_(n-1),1)>C_(n-1), and
balanced splits give C_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j for lambda=1+sqrt(2).
Selecting the split j=n-1 in B_n gives
B_n>=G(C_(n-1),1)>=2C_(n-1)-1. For d_N=floor(log_2 N),
both physical families obey n-1>=2^(d_N+3)-1, and consequently
B_n>=lambda^(d_N+3). Meanwhile M<=5N^2<20*4^(d_N). Hence
the common attained payoff satisfies

    B_n V >= [3lambda^3/(2*20^(7/12))]
                       (lambda/2^(7/6))^(d_N) >1.

This is an exact analytic estimate. Explicitly, lambda>12/5,
2^(7/6)<9/4 (equivalently 2^19<3^12), and 20^(7/12)<8
(use 5^7<2^21). Thus the prefactor is >324/125>2 and the
base is >16/15>1. The payoff exceeds U at EVERY claimed
N>=2^60 and the ratio diverges. No fitted profile constant, assumed
benchmark asymptotic, balanced-only defining recurrence, or DP was used.

For an arbitrary sufficiently large integer horizon n, use

    N=floor((n-3)/9),  k=n-(9N+3) in {0,...,8},
    or N=floor((n-4)/12), k=n-(12N+4) in {0,...,11}.

Insert k zeros at the VERY BEGINNING of the appropriate known word.
They repeat the already retained initial jet, so the exact full-prefix
maximum H is unchanged. The same static F has the same terminal value,
sharp smoothness and closest radius. The terminal remains unqueried.

For K, every new zero cut has numerator zero and every old cut's
preceding/following MASS is unchanged. Quadratic products acquire only
factors one. Lens_n shrinks. In the benchmark estimates use this
actual padded n, which still satisfies n<=16N for these large N
and has n-1 at least the unpadded value. Explicit sufficient start
horizons are 9*2^60+3 and 12*2^60+4. Thus the argument supplies
one selected attained word for each large n; it does not quantify over
every length-n word.

## 9. Precisely defined collapsed policy and its zero-tail boundary

This comparison retains the construction's p=e_1/M, initial point e_1,
first ordinary list and selected bridge AM+1. It treats ALL remaining
steps before the final bridge as one ordinary block, whose total is
M_2-1. Directly counting the original masses gives

    M_2=(B+2)M          for h_three,
    M_2=(B+C+3)M=(3B+4)M  for h_return.

Its specified static second slope is

    q_2=(-2e_1+sqrt(2A-4)e_2)/M_2,
    ||q_2||^2=2A/M_2^2.

For the static envelope over conv{0,p,q_2}, a first-stage proximal
point u=(1-t/M)e_1, 1<=t<=M, has p support (1-t/M)/M>=0,
q_2 support -2(1-t/M)/M_2<=0, and zero support0. Thus p is active
and x=u+p has projection p onto that triangle. The projection
regularity argument of Section 1 applies to this compact convex triangle
as well, proving that F_col_old is a legal member of the collapsed class.
After the first bridge, X=-Ae_1. At ordinary partial
mass s with t=1+s in [1,M_2],

    <q_2,X-tq_2>=(M_2-t)||q_2||^2>=0,
    <p,X-tq_2>=(-A+2t/M_2)/M<0.

Thus every physical query of THIS collapsed policy is legal, including
the old BM+1 and CM+1 steps now acting as individual ordinary entries.
Let y_2=X-M_2 q_2. The last queried jet is x=y_2+q_2,
g=q_2, f=||q_2||^2/2. For EVERY legal smooth-convex completion
of THIS entire prefix with its retained zero optimum, the descent
inequality at that jet gives

    f(y_2)<=f(y_2+q_2)-||q_2||^2/2=0.

Since the retained optimum has value zero, y_2 is forced to be a
minimum. The final physical step 1+M epsilon reaches
y_2-M epsilon q_2, and smoothness about this forced minimum gives

    2f(x_n)<=2A(M epsilon/M_2)^2.

The true radius for every such completion is also one, rather than
an assumed inherited denominator. The retained initial jet is
g_0=p and f_0=1/M-1/(2M^2). For any new minimizer x_star of value
zero, the original interpolation necessity from fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290 gives

    Q_(0,star)=f_0+<p,x_star-e_1>+||p||^2/2
             =x_star,1/M<=0.

So all new minima have first coordinate <=0, and the retained origin
is the unique closest one to e_1. This proves the bound for every
completion of that precise prefix with TRUE radius one.

When epsilon>0, the collapsed upper is positive and division is
legitimate. The three-stage witness-to-upper ratio is at least

    [B(B+2)^2/2](1-1/(2B^3)),

and the return witness-to-upper ratio is at least

    (3/4)(3B+4)^2.

Both factors exceed one and diverge on the chosen growing words,
where epsilon=M^(-1/3)>0. In the return parameter range epsilon>0
is automatic. Thus the substantive advertised growing-family comparison
holds, with exactly the stated collapsed policy as the comparison class.

At epsilon=0 in the three-stage parameter range, its formula in Section 3
gives V_three=0, and U_col_three=0. Every collapsed completion has nonnegative
terminal value and the upper bound zero, hence V_col(F_col)=0. Thus the weak
multiplicative inequality has equality at this boundary; its ratio is not
asserted. For epsilon>0, division by the explicitly positive upper comparison
U_col gives the two stated factors. For B>=2,
rho_col_three>=15>1 and rho_col_return>=75>1, so the new payoff is
strictly greater than every payoff in the respective collapsed class. A
particular collapsed completion may have payoff zero; no division by that
payoff is needed. In the return range epsilon>0 is forced by M epsilon>=2B^2.
On the specified growing words B=M^(1/120) tends to infinity, so both factors
tend to infinity. This comparison is confined to the exactly specified
prefix, second slope and retained optimum.

## 10. Original normalization and scope

For either attained function F, its minimum is0, the unique closest
minimizer to e_1 is0, the squared radius is1, and its least global gradient
Lipschitz constant is1. Thus V=2F(x_n) is exactly the project's W_n=2R_n
normalized payoff. Replacing F by L F for any L>0 makes the prescribed
updates x_next=x-(h_i/L)grad(L F)(x)=x-h_i grad F(x), with the same points
and the same payoff. Therefore W_n(h)>=V for the complete function class
in every stated case.

For each of the two specific padded word families, Sections 6--8 prove
V>U_n^ZJ at every integer horizon above its stated threshold, V/U_n^ZJ
tends to infinity, and the three exactly named reference channels are
o(U_n^ZJ), including the canonical Huber augmentation of the universal
lens. All padding counts and benchmark subscripts use actual physical
indices. These are selected-word lower witnesses and comparisons with
specified reference classes only. There is no bound on unrestricted W_n
from above, no extension to all nonlinear scalar witnesses or the full
complex legal region, and no arbitrary-word partition theorem. The original
arbitrary-word exact-profile theorem remains OPEN.
